I used to look forward to a lot of things... playing bridge, working out in the garden, helping with the cooking down at the church. I used to love going to Cubs games, too. But now, I just sit in my chair most of the day. I just can't seem to make myself care about any of it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find any pleasure in the things you're doing, even if it's just a little bit?\n\nPatient: No, not even a little bit. Like I was just saying, I really just sit in my chair all day now. There's no joy left in the gardening or the bridge games or any of it. I just... I can't make myself care about them at all anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you thought about how you would end your life if you decided to act on these thoughts?\n\nPatient: I haven't really figured out a specific way to do it. It's more that... well, I just keep thinking my wife and the kids would be better off if I simply didn't wake up. Lately, I've just been saying to myself that I don't need to see another winter.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or the way you move around since your bowling partner died?\n\nPatient: Well, yes. My wife says I move like I’m walking through deep snow these days. I just don't have the energy. Even simple things, like getting dressed in the morning or just crossing the kitchen... it takes me so long now. I usually just end up giving up before I’ve done much of anything at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight since your bowling partner died?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed. I... I don't step on the scale much these days. My clothes seem to fit about the same, I suppose, so if it has changed, I haven't paid any attention to it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty about anything in particular?\n\nPatient: Yes. I keep going over... well, every little thing I did wrong with him. And with my family, too. I just feel like I'm no good to anybody anymore. I really believe I deserve whatever punishment is coming to me.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way about being no good to anybody and deserving punishment?\n\nPatient: It's been going on since he died. That was... about a year and a month ago now. Everything just sort of started feeling that way after that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or fidgety, or have you noticed yourself sitting still for long periods without moving?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't been restless. It's more the opposite, really. My wife says I move like I'm walking through deep snow. Everything is just... slow. Even simple things, like getting dressed or just crossing the kitchen, it takes me so long now. I usually just end up giving up before I've really done much of anything and just sit there.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices or seeing things that other people can't?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. Some evenings... well, I hear his voice. Plain as day, coming from the hallway or the spare room. He tells me I should have been there, or that I failed him. My wife says the house is completely quiet, but I know what I hear.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been hearing your bowling partner's voice?\n\nPatient: Well, it started after I got really low about his passing. Before he died, and before I sank into this whole mess, I never heard his voice. I never believed I was being punished, either. All of that... it only started after I became so down and so guilty about everything. I've never had anything like this happen during the times in my life when I felt like my usual self. It's just been since I hit this bottom.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

I used to look forward to a lot of things... playing bridge, working in the garden, helping with the cooking down at the church. I even used to love going to Cubs games. But now, I just sit in my chair. But now, I just sit in my chair most of the day. Like I was just saying, I really just sit in my chair all day now. There's no joy left in the gardening or the bridge games or any of it. I just... I can't make myself care about them at all anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you thought about how you would end your life if you decided to act on these thoughts?\n\nPatient: I haven't really figured out a specific way to do it. It's more that... well, I just keep thinking my wife and the kids would be better off if I simply didn't wake up. Lately, I've just been saying to myself that I don't need to see another winter.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or the way you move around since your bowling partner died?\n\nPatient: Well, yes. My wife says I move like I’m walking through deep snow these days. I just don't have the energy. I... I don't step on the scale much these days. My clothes seem to fit about the same, I suppose, so if it has changed, I haven't paid any attention to it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty about anything in particular?\n\nPatient: Yes. I keep going over... well, every little thing I did wrong with him. And with my family, too. I just feel like I'm no good to anybody anymore.
